Pairs with common neighbor:
- (1,3): share common neighbor 2
- (2,4): share common neighbor 3  
- Others: (1,2), (1,4), (2,3), (3,4) - are they with common neighbor?

(1,2): directly connected
(1,4): no common neighbor? Common neighbor of 1 and 4: w adjacent to 1 and w adjacent to 4. 1's neighbors: {2}, 4's neighbors: {3}. No common neighbor.
(2,3): directly connected  
(3,4): directly connected

So only (1,3) and (2,4) have common neighbors.

Pairs with common neighbor: 2 pairs
Pairs without common neighbor: (1,2), (1,4), (2,3), (3,4) → 4 pairs

But in new graph:
- (1,2): distance 1
- (1,3): distance 1
- (1,4): distance 2  
- (2,3): distance 1
- (2,4): distance 1
- (3,4): distance 1

So sum = 1+1+2+1+1+1 = 7

The pairs without common neighbor: (1,4) has distance 2, others have distance 1.

So the rule is:
- If a pair has a common neighbor in original tree, distance = 1 in new graph
- If a pair does not have a common neighbor in original tree, distance = 2 if they are at distance 2 or more in original, but distance = 1 if they are at distance 1

For pairs at distance 1 in original: they are directly connected, so distance = 1 in new graph
For pairs at distance 2 in original: if they have common neighbor, distance = 1; else distance = 2
For pairs at distance > 2: distance = 2 in new graph (since they can reach through common neighbors)

In example 2, (1,4) are at distance 3 in original. Do they have a common neighbor? No. So distance = 2 in new graph.

In path 1-2-3-4:
- (1,4): distance 3 in original, no common neighbor, distance 2 in new

So general rule:
- If distance between u,v in original tree is 1: distance in new graph = 1
- If distance between u,v in original tree is > 1: distance in new graph = 1 if they have common neighbor, else 2

But distance > 1 means they are not directly connected.

So let's define:
- For each pair (u,v):
  - if u and v are adjacent in tree: distance = 1
  - else: if they have common neighbor: distance = 1, else distance = 2

So total sum = sum over all pairs of:
  - 1 if (u,v) are adjacent or have common neighbor
  - 2 if (u,v) are not adjacent and don't have common neighbor

This can be computed as:
- Sum = (number of adjacent pairs) * 1 + (number of pairs with common neighbor) * 1 + (number of non-adjacent pairs without common neighbor) * 2
- = (number of adjacent pairs) + (number of pairs with common neighbor) + 2 * (number of non-adjacent pairs without common neighbor)

Number of adjacent pairs = n-1

Number of pairs with common neighbor = sum over w of C(deg(w),2)

Total pairs = C(n,2)

Number of non-adjacent pairs = C(n,2) - (n-1)

Number of non-adjacent pairs without common neighbor = (C(n,2) - (n-1)) - sum_w C(deg(w),2)

So sum = (n-1) + sum_w C(deg(w),2) + 2 * ((n*(n-1)/2 - (n-1)) - sum_w C(deg(w),2))
= (n-1) + sum C(deg(w),2) + 2 * (n*(n-1)/2 - n + 1 - sum C(deg(w),2))
= (n-1) + sum C(deg(w),2) + (n*(n-1) - 2n + 2) - 2 * sum C(deg(w),2)
= (n-1) + (n*(n-1) - 2n + 2) - sum C(deg(w),2)
= n*(n-1) - n - 1 + 1 - sum C(deg(w),2)
= n*(n-2) - sum C(deg(w),2)

Let me verify with examples:

Example 1: n=4, sum C(deg(w),2) = C(3,2) + 3*C(1,2) = 3 + 0 = 3
Sum = 4*2 - 3 = 8 - 3 = 5 ≠ 6

Wrong again.

Let me try to recalculate everything carefully.

Example 1:
- Adjacent pairs: (1,2), (1,3), (1,4) → 3 pairs, distance = 1
- Pairs with common neighbor: (2,3), (2,4), (3,4) → 3 pairs, distance = 1
- Total pairs: C(4,2)=6
- All pairs have distance 1, sum = 6

Sum = 3*1 + 3*1 = 6

Example 2:
- Adjacent pairs: (1,2), (2,3), (3,4) → 3 pairs, distance = 1
- Pairs with common neighbor: (1,3), (2,4) → 2 pairs, distance = 1
- Pair (1,4): not adjacent, no common neighbor, distance = 2
- Sum = 3*1 + 2*1 + 1*2 = 3+2+2 = 7

So in general:
Sum = (number of adjacent pairs) * 1 + (number of non-adjacent pairs with common neighbor) * 1 + (number of non-adjacent pairs without common neighbor) * 2

For non-adjacent pairs with common neighbor: sum_w C(deg(w),2)
For non-adjacent pairs without common neighbor: C(n,2) - (n-1) - sum_w C(deg(w),2)

Sum = (n-1) + sum_w C(deg(w),2) + 2 * (C(n,2) - (n-1) - sum_w C(deg(w),2))
= (n-1) + sum_w C(deg(w),2) + 2*(n*(n-1)/2 - (n-1) - sum_w C(deg(w),2))
= (n-1) + sum_w C(deg(w),2) + (n*(n-1) - 2*(n-1) - 2*sum_w C(deg(w),2))
= (n-1) + sum_w C(deg(w),2) + n*(n-1) - 2*(n-1) - 2*sum_w C(deg(w),2)
= (n-1) + n*(n-1) - 2*(n-1) - sum_w C(deg(w),2)
= (n-1)*(1 + n - 2) - sum_w C(deg(w),2)
= (n-1)*(n-1) - sum_w C(deg(w),2)
= (n-1)^2 - sum_w C(deg(w),2)

Example 1: n=4, (4-1)^2 - 3 = 9 - 3 = 6 ✓
Example 2: n=4, (4-1)^2 - [C(2,2)+C(2,2)+C(1,2)+C(1,2)] = 9 - [1+1+0+0] = 9 - 2 = 7 ✓

Perfect! This works.

Therefore, the solution is:
1. Read input and build adjacency list
2. For each node w, compute C(deg(w),2)
3. Sum all C(deg(w),2)
4. Answer = (n-1)^2 - sum of C(deg(w),2)

```python
n = int(input())
if n == 1:
    print(0)
else:
    # Build adjacency list
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n+1)]
    for _ in range(n-1):
        u, v = map(int, input().split())
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
    
    # Calculate sum of C(deg(w), 2) for all nodes w
    total_c = 0
    for w in range(1, n+1):
        deg = len(adj[w])
        if deg >= 2:
            total_c += deg * (deg - 1) // 2
    
    # Answer is (n-1)^2 - total_c
    result = (n - 1) * (n - 1) - total_c
    print(result)
```
